For
e=\{v_{1},v_{2}\}\in E(G)
, we have
e\in V(G^{\prime})
.
If
v_{1}<v_{2}
,
then it holds that
G^{\prime}\models\varphi_{E,1}[v_{1},e]\land\varphi_{E,2}[v_{2},e]
.
If
v_{2}<v_{1}
,
then we have
G^{\prime}\models\varphi_{E,1}[v_{2},e]\land\varphi_{E,2}[v_{1},e]
.
This shows that
G^{\prime}\models\varphi_{E}[v_{1},v_{2}]

and
G^{\prime}\models\varphi_{E}[v_{2},v_{1}]
.
It can be easily verified that
G^{\prime}\not\models\varphi_{E}[v_{1},v_{2}]

for all
v_{1},v_{2}\in E(G)
with
\{v_{1},v_{2}\}\not\in E(G)
.

This shows that
G
is the result of applying the transduction
(\varphi_{V},\varphi_{E})
on
G^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
G^{\prime}
can be computed from
G
in polynomial time.

Finally, we show that the class of
\{E\}
-reducts of the resulting coloured graphs
G^{\prime}

has shrub-depth at most
2
.
As defined in
[
18
]
,
a class of graphs has shrub-depth at most
d

if there is a number
m
of labels such that for every graph
G^{\prime}
in the class,
there is a tree
T
with the following properties.

1.

The set of leaves of
T
is exactly
V(G^{\prime})
.

2.

The length of each root-to-leaf path in
T
is exactly
d
.

3.

Each leaf of
T
is assigned one of
m
labels.

4.

The existence of an edge
\{v_{1},v_{2}\}\in E(G^{\prime})

depends solely on the labels of
v_{1}
and
v_{2}

and the distance between
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
in
T
.

We show that the
\{E\}
-reduct of every coloured graph
G^{\prime}
resulting from our construction above
can be defined via such a tree with
2
labels.
For this, we let the leaves of the tree
T
be

V(G^{\prime})=V(G)\cup E(G)\cup V_{V}\cup V_{E,1}\cup V_{E,2}
.
The leaves in
V(G)\cup E(G)
are given the label
1
,
and the leaves in
V_{V}\cup V_{E,1}\cup V_{E,2}
are given the label
2
.
In addition to the leaves,
we let
V(T)
contain a fresh root node
r

as well as a fresh node
i^{\prime}
for every
i\in V(G)

and a fresh node
e^{\prime}
for every
e\in E(G)
.
We let the fresh nodes
i^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}
be the children of
r
.
For a node
i^{\prime}
with
i\in V(G)
,
we let the children of
i^{\prime}
be the vertices in
V_{i}
as well as the vertex
i
.
For a node
e^{\prime}
with
e\in V(G)
,
we let the children of
e^{\prime}
be the vertices in
V_{e,1}\cup V_{e,2}

as well as the vertex
e
.
The edges of
G^{\prime}
are exactly the pairs of vertices
of distance
2
in
T
with labels
1
and
2

(these are the connections between the vertices in
V(G)\cup E(G)

with their
- and
-neighbours)
and the pairs of vertices of distance
4
in
T
with labels
1
and
1

(this is the clique on
V(G)\cup E(G)
).

Appendix D
Proofs Omitted in Section
4

D.1
Proof of Lemma
15

For proving
Lemma

15
,
we show the following result for
\mathsf{FO}
instead of
\mathsf{cgFOC}
.

Lemma 37
.

There is a computable function
T\colon\mathsf{FO}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
such that,
for every
\mathsf{FO}
formula
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})
,
every
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
every
\sigma(\varphi)
-structure
\mathcal{A}
,
for
r\coloneqq 7^{\qr(\varphi)}
,
and for all
V,W\subseteq A
that are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
by a set of size at most
p
,
we have
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(\varphi,p)

for
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}}.{\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
.

Before we prove
Lemma

37
,
we first show how to obtain
Lemma

15
from it.

See
15

Proof.

Let
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
be a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
counting term,
let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\sigma
-structure for some
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(t)
,
and let
V,W\subseteq A
be sets that are
r(t)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}

by a set of size at most
m
,
where
r\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N}
will be specified below.

If
\sigma\neq\sigma(t)
,
then we let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the
\sigma(t)
-reduct of
\mathcal{A}
.
It holds that
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)=M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V/W)
,
and
V
and
W
are also
r(t)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}

by a set of size at most
m
.
Thus, it suffices to provide a computable upper bound
T(t,m)

for
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V/W)\bigr)
,
since the same value is also an upper bound for
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)
.
Hence, in the following, we may assume that
\sigma=\sigma(t)
.

We prove the result by structural induction on
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
.
As a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
counting term,
t
is the result of applying
rule (
21
),
(
21
),
or (
21
)
of
Definition

27
.

If
t
is the result of applying rule (
21
),
then it is a #-term of the form

t(\bar{x},\bar{y})=\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
for a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
formula
\psi
.
We apply
Lemma

7
to
\mathcal{A}
and
\psi
,
and we obtain a signature
\sigma_{\psi}\supseteq\sigma
,
an
\mathsf{FO}[\sigma_{\psi}]
formula
\psi^{\prime}
,
and a
\sigma_{\psi}
-expansion
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}
of
\mathcal{A}
with
G_{\mathcal{A}}=G_{\mathcal{A}_{\psi}}

such that for all
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
,
\bar{w}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}
,
and
\bar{c}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert}
,
we have
\mathcal{A}\models\psi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]
if and only if
\mathcal{A}_{\psi}\models\psi^{\prime}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]
.
Hence, for
t^{\prime}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi^{\prime}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
,
it holds that
\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=\llbracket t^{\prime}(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}_{\psi}}
,
so
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)=M^{t^{\prime}}_{\!\mathcal{A}_{\psi}}(V/W)
.
Let
T_{\mathsf{FO}}\colon\mathsf{FO}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
be the computable function from
Lemma

37
.
We set
r(t)\coloneqq(2\cdot 7^{\qr(\psi^{\prime})}+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)

and
T(t,m)\coloneqq T_{\mathsf{FO}}(\psi^{\prime},m)
.
Note that, since
\psi^{\prime}
is computable from
t
,

r(t)
is also computable from
t
,
and
T(t,m)
is computable from
t
and
m
.
By
Lemma

37
, since
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{FO}
,
and since
V
and
W
are
r(t)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
by a set of size at most
m

if and only if they are
r(t)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
by a set of size at most
m
,
we have

\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)=\rank\bigl(M^{t^{\prime}}_{\!\mathcal{A}_{\psi}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T_{\mathsf{FO}}(\psi^{\prime},m)=T(t,m)
.

If
t
is the result of applying rule (
21
),
then
t=i
for some
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Hence,
\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)_{\bar{v},\bar{w}}=i

for all
\bar{v}\in V^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
and
\bar{w}\in W^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}
.
Thus, we have
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq 1
,
and we do not rely on
V
and
W
being separated,
so we can set
r(t)\coloneqq 0
and
T(t,m)\coloneqq 1
.

Lastly, if
t
is the result of applying rule (
21
),
then
t=t_{1}+t_{2}
or
t=t_{1}\cdot t_{2}
for
\mathsf{cgFOC}
counting terms
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
.
By the induction hypothesis, for
i\in[2]
,

r(t_{i})
is computable from
t_{i}
and

T(t_{i},m)
is computable from
t_{i}
and
m

such that
\rank\bigl(M^{t_{i}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(t_{i},m)

for all sets
V,W\subseteq A
that are
r(t_{i})
-separated by a set of size at most
m
.
We set
r(t)\coloneqq\max\{r(t_{1}),r(t_{2})\}
.
If
t=t_{1}+t_{2}
, then
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)=M^{t_{1}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)+M^{t_{2}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
, so

\displaystyle\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\rank\bigl(M^{t_{1}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)+\rank\bigl(M^{t_{2}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq T(t_{1},m)+T(t_{2},m)\eqqcolon T(t,m).

If
t=t_{1}\cdot t_{2}
, then
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)=M^{t_{1}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\odot M^{t_{2}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
,
where
\odot
denotes the element-wise multiplication of two matrices,
for which it holds that
\rank(M_{1}\odot M_{2})\leq\rank(M_{1})\cdot\rank(M_{2})

for matrices
M_{1},M_{2}
(see,
e.g.
,
[
25
, Theorem 5.1.7]
).
Thus,

\displaystyle\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\rank\bigl(M^{t_{1}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\cdot\rank\bigl(M^{t_{2}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq T(t_{1},m)\cdot T(t_{2},m)\eqqcolon T(t,m).

All in all,
T
and
r
as defined above are suitable computable functions,
and this concludes the proof.
∎

Towards
Lemma

37
, we first prove the following result,
where the formula
\delta^{\sigma}_{G,r}
is defined as in

Section

C.4
.

Lemma 38
.

There is a computable function
T\colon\mathsf{FO}\to\mathbb{N}
such that, for every
\mathsf{FO}
formula

\psi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})=\delta^{\sigma(\varphi)}_{G,2r+1}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\land\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})

for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,

G\in\mathcal{G}_{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert}
,
and an
r
-local
\mathsf{FO}
formula
\varphi
,
for every
\sigma(\varphi)
-structure
\mathcal{A}
,
and all
V,W\subseteq A
that are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
,
we have
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(\psi)

for
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
.

Proof.

Let
k\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert
,
\ell\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert
, and
m\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert
.
If
k=0
, then
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
only has a single row,
so
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq 1\eqqcolon T(\psi)

for all
V,W\subseteq A
.
Analogously, if
\ell=0
, then
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
only has a single column,
and we again have
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq 1\eqqcolon T(\psi)

for all
V,W\subseteq A
.
Thus, in the following, we assume
k>0
and
\ell>0
.

We prove the result by induction on the number of connected components of
G
.
For the base case, let
G
be a connected graph.
Then it holds that
\mathcal{A}\not\models\delta^{\sigma(\varphi)}_{G,2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]

for all
\bar{v}\in V^{k}
,
\bar{w}\in W^{\ell}
, and
\bar{c}\in A^{m}
,
since
V
and
W
are
\bigl((2r+1)(m+1)\bigr)
-separated,
which implies that
\dist^{A}(\bar{v},\bar{w})>(2r+1)(m+1)
.
Thus, we have
\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=0
for all
\bar{v}\in V^{k}
and
\bar{w}\in W^{\ell}
,
so
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)=0\eqqcolon T(\psi)
.

Now suppose that
G
has
p>1
connected components,
and suppose the statement of
Lemma

38
holds for all graphs
G

with at most
p-1
connected components.

If there is a connected component of
G
that has a non-empty intersection
with both
[k]
and
[k+1,\ell]
,
then, analogously to the case that
G
is connected,
it holds that
\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=0
for all
\bar{v}\in V^{k}
and
\bar{w}\in W^{\ell}
,
and
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)=0\eqqcolon T(\psi)
.

Otherwise, let
V_{1}\subseteq V(G)
be the vertices of all connected components of
G

that contain a vertex from
[k]
,
and let
V_{2}\coloneqq V(G)\setminus V_{1}
.
Then
[k]\subseteq V_{1}\subseteq[k]\cup[k+\ell+1,k+\ell+m]
,

[k+1,\ell]\subseteq V_{2}\subseteq[k+1,k+\ell+m]
,
and
G_{1}\coloneqq G[V_{1}]
and
G_{2}\coloneqq G[V_{2}]
have at most
p-1
connected components.
The following argumentation is similar to the one in the proof of
Lemma

35
.

For
i\in[2]
, we let
\bar{z}_{i}
be the tuple obtained from
\bar{z}

by keeping only those entries at index
j
with
k+\ell+j\in V_{i}
,
we analogously define
\bar{c}_{i}
for a tuple
\bar{c}\in A^{m}
,
and we set
m_{i}\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{z}_{i}\right\rvert
,

Let
\Delta
be a Feferman–Vaught decomposition of
\varphi
with respect to
V_{1}
and
V_{2}

computed via
Theorem

22
.
We let
\Delta^{\prime}
be the set containing the pair
(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})

for every
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\Delta
,
where
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
is obtained from
\alpha_{i}

by recursively replacing subformulas of the form
\exists x\varphi
by

\exists x\bigl(\varphi\land\bigvee_{y\in\free(\alpha_{i})}\dist^{\sigma}_{\leq r}(x,y)\bigr)
.
Then
\Delta^{\prime}
is computable from
\psi
,
V_{1}
, and
V_{2}
,
and every formula occurring in
\Delta^{\prime}
is
r
-local.

Thus, for
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
,
\bar{w}\in A^{\ell}
, and
\bar{c}\in A^{m}
with

\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{w}\bar{c})=\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\uplus\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})
,
it holds that
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{w}\bar{c})\models\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]

if and only if there is some
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\Delta

with
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\models\alpha_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]

and
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})\models\alpha_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]
,
and this holds if and only if there is some
(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}

such that
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\models\alpha^{\prime}_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]

and
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})\models\alpha^{\prime}_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]
.
Since
\varphi
and the formulas in
\Delta^{\prime}
are
r
-local,
this implies that
\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]

if and only if there is some
(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}

such that
\mathcal{A}\models\alpha^{\prime}_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]

and
\mathcal{A}\models\alpha^{\prime}_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]
.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G,2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]

implies that
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{w}\bar{c})=\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\uplus\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})
.

This shows that, for all
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
,
\bar{w}\in A^{\ell}
,
\bar{c}\in A^{m}
,
we have
\mathcal{A}\models\psi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]

if and only if

\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{w}\bar{c})=\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\uplus\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})

and there is some
(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}

such that
\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{1},2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]

and
\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{2},2r+1}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]
.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{G}_{k+\ell+m}
be the class of graphs
G^{\prime}

with
G^{\prime}[V_{1}]=G_{1}
,
G^{\prime}[V_{2}]=G_{2}
, and
G^{\prime}\neq G
.
For every such graph
G^{\prime}
, there has to be an edge between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
,
so
G^{\prime}
has at most
p-1
connected components.
Moreover, for every
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
,
\bar{w}\in A^{\ell}
, and
\bar{c}\in A^{m}

with
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{w}\bar{c})\neq\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\cup\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})

(that is, there is an additional edge in
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{w}\bar{c})

between a vertex from
N_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})
and a vertex from
N_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})
),

\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}\bar{c}_{1})\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{1},2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]
,
and
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{w}\bar{c}_{2})\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{2},2r+1}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]
,
it holds that
\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G^{\prime}\!,2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]

for some
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

By definition, we have

\llbracket\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi(\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{z})}\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}\in A^{m}:\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G,2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]\land\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert.

Since the formulas in
\Delta^{\prime}
are mutually exclusive, this is equal to

\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}}\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}\in A^{m}:\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G,2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert.

Lastly, by the definition of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
above, the term is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}}\Bigl(\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}_{1}\in A^{m_{1}}:\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{1},2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\mkern 60.0mu\cdot\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}_{2}\in A^{m_{2}}:\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{2},2r+1}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle-\sum_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}\in A^{m}:\mathcal{A}\models\delta^{\sigma}_{G^{\prime}\!,2r+1}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{1}[\bar{v},\bar{c}_{1}]\land\alpha^{\prime}_{2}[\bar{w},\bar{c}_{2}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert\Bigr).

For every
(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}
, we set

\displaystyle\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}(\bar{x},\bar{z}_{1})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{1},2r+1}(\bar{x},\bar{z}_{1})\land\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(\bar{x},\bar{z}_{1}),

\displaystyle\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}(\bar{y},\bar{z}_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\delta^{\sigma}_{G_{2},2r+1}(\bar{y},\bar{z}_{2})\land\alpha^{\prime}_{2}(\bar{y},\bar{z}_{2}),

and we define

t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}(\bar{x})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}_{1}}.{\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}(\bar{x},\bar{z}_{1})}
,

t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}(\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}_{2}}.{\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}(\bar{y},\bar{z}_{2})}
,

u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}(\bar{x})
,
and

u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}(\bar{y})
.
Furthermore, for all
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, we set

\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\coloneqq\delta^{\sigma}_{G^{\prime}\!,2r+1}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\land\alpha^{\prime}_{1}(\bar{x},\bar{z}_{1})\land\alpha^{\prime}_{2}(\bar{y},\bar{z}_{2})

and

t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
.
Then

\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}}\Bigl(\llbracket u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\llbracket u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}-\sum_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\llbracket t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}\Bigr)

for all
\bar{v}\in V^{k}
and
\bar{w}\in W^{\ell}
.
Thus,

M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)=\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}}\Bigl(M^{u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\odot M^{u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)-\sum_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}M^{t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\Bigr),

where
\odot
denotes the element-wise multiplication of two matrices.
By the induction hypothesis applied to
\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}
,
\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}
,
and
\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}
,
since
G_{1}
,
G_{2}
, and
G^{\prime}
have at most
p-1
connected components
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{1}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{2}
are
r
-local,
it holds that

\rank\bigl(M^{u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{i}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{i}})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
and

\rank\bigl(M^{t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}})
.
Hence,
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)
is at most

\displaystyle\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}}\Bigl(\rank\bigl(M^{u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\cdot\rank\bigl(M^{u_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)+\sum_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\rank\bigl(M^{t_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sum_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2})\in\Delta^{\prime}}\Bigl(T(\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}})\cdot T(\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}})+\sum_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}T(\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime}_{2},G^{\prime}})\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\ T(\psi).

Since
\Delta^{\prime}
is computable from
\psi

and, by the induction hypothesis,

T(\psi^{\prime})
is computable from
\psi^{\prime}

for all
\psi^{\prime}
defined via a graph
G^{\prime}
with at most
p-1
connected components,

T(\psi)
is computable from
\psi
.

All in all, this shows that
T
as defined above is a suitable computable function,
and this concludes the proof.
∎

With
Lemma

38
at hand, we can now prove
Lemma

37
.

Proof of
Lemma

37
.

Let
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\in\mathsf{FO}
be an
\mathsf{FO}
formula,
let
k\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert
,
\ell\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert
,
m\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert
,
let
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}}.{\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
,
and
r\coloneqq 7^{\qr(\varphi)}
.
In order to prove
Lemma

37
,
we define computable values
T(\varphi,p)
such that, for every
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
every
\sigma(\varphi)
-structure
\mathcal{A}
,
and all
V,W\subseteq A
that are
\bigl((2r+1)(m+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}

by a set of size at most
p
,
we have
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(\varphi,p)
.

The cases
k=0
and
\ell=0
can be handled analogously
to the corresponding cases in the proof of
Lemma

38

by setting
T(\varphi,p)\coloneqq 1
.
Hence, in the following, we assume
k>0
and
\ell>0
.

Using
Theorem

24
,
we transform
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})
into an equivalent formula

\psi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})
in Gaifman normal form with radius at most
r
.

In the following, by induction on
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
we define
T(\varphi,p)
such that
T
is computable
and has the properties mentioned above.
Consider the base case
p=0
.
Let
\Psi
be the set of all formulas obtained from
\psi

by replacing every basic local sentence occurring in
\psi
by
\top
or
\bot
.
Then every formula in
\Psi
is
r
-local,
and
\Psi
is a finite set that is computable from
\varphi
.
Let
\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\mathcal{G}_{k+\ell+m}
.
For every formula
\psi^{\prime}
in
\Psi
and every graph
G\in\mathcal{G}
,
we let
\psi^{\prime}_{G}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\coloneqq\delta^{\sigma(\varphi)}_{G,2r+1}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\land\psi^{\prime}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})

and
t_{\psi^{\prime},G}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi^{\prime}_{G}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
.

Let
T_{\ref*{lem:fo-dist-rank}}
be the computable function from
Lemma

38
.
For every
\sigma(\varphi)
-structure
\mathcal{A}
and for all sets
V,W\subseteq A

that are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
, it holds that

\rank\bigl(M^{t_{\psi^{\prime},G}}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T_{\ref*{lem:fo-dist-rank}}(\psi^{\prime}_{G})
.
Let

T(\varphi,0)\coloneqq\max_{\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi}\sum_{G\in\mathcal{G}}T_{\ref*{lem:fo-dist-rank}}(\psi^{\prime}_{G})
,
which is computable from
\varphi
.

For a
\sigma(\varphi)
-structure
\mathcal{A}
,
we let
\psi_{\mathcal{A}}\in\Psi
be the formula obtained from
\psi

where we replace every basic local sentence
\chi
occurring in
\psi

by
\top
if
\mathcal{A}\models\chi
,
and we replace it by
\bot
if
\mathcal{A}\not\models\chi
.
Then
\psi_{\mathcal{A}}\in\Psi
.
Furthermore, for all
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
,
\bar{w}\in A^{\ell}
, and
\bar{c}\in A^{m}
,
it holds that
\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]
if and only if

\mathcal{A}\models\psi_{\mathcal{A}}[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]
.
Moreover, for
\psi_{\mathcal{A},G}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})=\delta^{\sigma(\varphi)}_{G,2r+1}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})\land\psi_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})

and
t_{\mathcal{A},G}(\bar{x},\bar{y})=\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi_{\mathcal{A},G}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z})}
,
we have

\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=\sum_{G\in\mathcal{G}}\llbracket t_{\mathcal{A},G}(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}
.

Let
V,W\subseteq A
be two sets that are

\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Then it holds that

\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq\sum_{G\in\mathcal{G}}T_{\ref*{lem:fo-dist-rank}}(\psi_{\mathcal{A},G})\leq T(\varphi,0)
.
This finishes the proof for
p=0
.

Now let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
and suppose the result holds for all
p^{\prime}<p
.
Let
V,W\subseteq A
be two sets
that are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}

by a set
S\subseteq A
of size at most
p
.
If
\left\lvert S\right\rvert<p
, then the result already holds by induction hypothesis,
so suppose
\left\lvert S\right\rvert=p
with
S=\{s_{1},\dots,s_{p}\}
.

Intuitively, in the following, we remove
s_{p}
from
\mathcal{A}
,
and we encode the tuples in relations of
\mathcal{A}
involving
s_{p}

by using new relation symbols.
We call the resulting structure
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
The values in
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
will correspond to values in

M^{t^{\prime}}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime})
for

V^{\prime}\coloneqq V\setminus\{s_{p}\}
,

W^{\prime}\coloneqq W\setminus\{s_{p}\}
,
and for counting terms
t^{\prime}
that can be computed from
t
.
Hence, to bound the rank of
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
,
it suffices to bound the rank of
M^{t^{\prime}}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime})
.
Since
V^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}
are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}

by the set
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{p-1}\}
of size
p-1
,
we can provide a computable bound by the induction hypothesis.

Formally, we proceed as follows.
We let
\sigma^{\prime}
be the signature obtained from
\sigma

by replacing every relation symbol
R\in\sigma

by fresh relation symbols
R_{f}
, for every
f\colon[\ar(R)]\to\{0,1\}
,
of arity
\ar(R_{f})\coloneqq\left\lvert\{i\in[\ar(R)]:f(i)=0\}\right\rvert
.
We let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the
\sigma^{\prime}
-structure obtained from
\mathcal{A}

with
A^{\prime}\coloneqq A\setminus\{s_{p}\}
by letting

R_{f}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
contain all tuples that are obtained from a tuple
\bar{v}\in R(\mathcal{A})
,
with
v_{i}=s_{p}
if and only if
f(i)=1
,
by dropping all entries from
\bar{v}
that are equal to
s_{p}

(or, equivalently, all entries with indices
i
where
f(i)=1
).
For every formula
\psi\in\mathsf{FO}
and every function
g\colon\free(\psi)\to\{0,1\}
,
we let
\psi_{g}
be the formula obtained from
\psi
as follows.

•

If
\psi
is of the form
z_{1}=z_{2}
and

–

g(z_{1})=g(z_{2})=0
, then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq z_{1}=z_{2}
;

–

g(z_{1})=g(z_{2})=1
, then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq\top
;

–

g(z_{1})\neq g(z_{2})
, then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq\bot
.

•

If
\psi
is of the form
R(\bar{z})
,
then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq R_{h}(\bar{z}_{g})
,
where
h\colon[\ar(R)]\to\{0,1\}

with
h(i)=0
if and only if
g(z_{i})=0
,
and
\bar{z}_{g}
is obtained from
\bar{z}
by dropping all variables
z_{i}

with
g(z_{i})=1
.

•

If
\psi
is of the form
\neg\psi^{\prime}
,
then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq\neg\psi^{\prime}_{g}
.

•

If
\psi
is of the form
\psi^{\prime}\lor\psi^{\prime\prime}
,
then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq\psi^{\prime}_{g}\lor\psi^{\prime\prime}_{g}
.

•

If
\psi
is of the form
\exists x\,\psi^{\prime}
,
then we set
\psi_{g}\coloneqq\psi^{\prime}_{g^{\prime}}\lor\exists x\,\psi^{\prime}_{g^{\prime\prime}}
,
where
g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}\colon\free(\psi)\uplus\{x\}\to\{0,1\}
,

g^{\prime}(z)\coloneqq g^{\prime\prime}(z)\coloneqq g(z)
for all
z\in\free(\psi)

and
g^{\prime}(x)\coloneqq 1
and
g^{\prime\prime}(x)\coloneqq 0
.

All in all, it holds that
\free(\psi_{g})\subseteq\{x\in\free(\psi):g(x)=0\}

and
\qr(\psi_{g})=\qr(\psi)
.
Moreover, for every formula
\psi(z_{1},\dots,z_{q})\in\mathsf{FO}[\sigma]

and every tuple
\bar{v}\in A^{q}
,
it holds that
\mathcal{A}\models\psi[\bar{v}]
if and only if

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\models\psi_{g}[\bar{v}_{g}]
,
where
g\colon\free(\psi)\to\{0,1\}
,
with
g(z_{i})=1
if and only if
v_{i}=s_{p}
,
and
\bar{v}_{g}
is obtained from
\bar{v}
by dropping all indices
i

with
g(z_{i})=1
, or, equivalently, all entries that are equal to
s_{p}
.
In addition, we have
G_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}=G_{\mathcal{A}}[A^{\prime}]=G_{\mathcal{A}}[A\setminus\{s_{p}\}]
.

Let
F
be the set of all functions
f\colon\free(\varphi)\to\{0,1\}
.
We set
T(\varphi,p)\coloneqq\sum_{f\in F}T(\varphi_{f},p-1)
.
By the induction hypothesis, the values
T(\varphi_{f},p-1)
are computable,
so
T(\varphi,p)
is computable as well.

Now let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\sigma(\varphi)
-structure,
and let
V,W\subseteq A
be two sets that are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
by a set
S=\{s_{1},\dots,s_{p}\}\subseteq A
of size
\left\lvert S\right\rvert=p
.
We shall prove that
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(\varphi,p)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the
\sigma^{\prime}
-structure based on
\mathcal{A}
and
s_{p}
as described above.
Then
V^{\prime}\coloneqq V\setminus\{s_{p}\}
and
W^{\prime}\coloneqq W\setminus\{s_{p}\}

are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}

by the set
S^{\prime}\coloneqq\{s_{1},\dots,s_{p-1}\}
of size
p-1
.

Let
F_{x}
be the set of functions
f_{x}\colon\tilde{x}\to\{0,1\}
,
let
F_{y}
be the set of functions
f_{y}\colon\tilde{y}\to\{0,1\}
,
and let
F_{z}
be the set of functions
f_{z}\colon\tilde{z}\to\{0,1\}
.
For every
f_{z}\in F_{z}
,
we let

A^{m}_{f_{z}}\coloneqq\bigl\{\bar{c}\in A^{m}:c_{i}=s_{p}\iff f_{z}(z_{i})=1\bigr\},

and we let
M_{f_{z}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{V^{k}\times W^{\ell}}
with

(M_{f_{z}})_{\bar{v},\bar{w}}\coloneqq\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}\in A^{m}_{f_{z}}:\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert.

Then
A^{m}=\biguplus_{f_{z}\in F_{z}}A^{m}_{f_{z}}
and
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)=\sum_{f_{z}\in F_{z}}M_{f_{z}}
.
This shows that

\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq\sum_{f_{z}\in F_{z}}\rank(M_{f_{z}})
.

For
f_{x}\in F_{x}
,
f_{y}\in F_{y}
, and
f_{z}\in F_{z}
,
let
V^{k}_{f_{x}}\coloneqq\bigl\{\bar{v}\in V^{k}:v_{i}=s_{p}\iff f_{x}(x_{i})=1\bigr\}
,

W^{\ell}_{f_{y}}\coloneqq\bigl\{\bar{w}\in W^{\ell}:w_{i}=s_{p}\iff f_{y}(y_{i})=1\bigr\}
,
and let
M_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{V^{k}_{f_{x}}\times W^{\ell}_{f_{y}}}
with

(M_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}})_{\bar{v},\bar{w}}\coloneqq\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bar{c}\in A^{m}_{f_{z}}:\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{c}]\bigr\}\bigr\rvert.

Let

\varphi_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}}(\bar{x}_{f},\bar{y}_{f},\bar{z}_{f})\coloneqq\varphi_{f}(\bar{x}_{f},\bar{y}_{f},\bar{z}_{f})

for the function
f\in F

with
f(x_{i})=f_{x}(x_{i})
,
f(y_{i})=f_{y}(y_{i})
, and
f(z_{i})=f_{z}(z_{i})
,
and let

t_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}}(\bar{x}_{f},\bar{y}_{f})\coloneqq t_{f}(\bar{x}_{f},\bar{y}_{f})\coloneqq\#{\bar{z}_{f}}.{\varphi_{f}(\bar{x}_{f},\bar{y}_{f},\bar{z}_{f})}
.
Then
(M_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}})_{\bar{v},\bar{w}}=\bigl(M^{t_{f}}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime})\bigr)_{\bar{v}_{f},\bar{w}_{f}}
,
so
\rank(M_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}})=\rank\bigl(M^{t_{f}}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime})\bigr)
.
Moreover, by the induction hypothesis, since
V^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}

are
\bigl((2r+1)(\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert+1)\bigr)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
by a set of size at most
p-1
,
and
\qr(\varphi_{f})=\qr(\varphi)
and
\left\lvert\bar{z}_{f}\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert\bar{z}\right\rvert
,
we have
\rank\bigl(M^{t_{f}}_{\!\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(V^{\prime}/W^{\prime})\bigr)\leq T(\varphi_{f},p-1)
.
Lastly,
M_{f_{z}}
consists of the submatrices
M_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}}

for all
f_{x}\in F_{x}
and
f_{y}\in F_{y}
,
so
\rank(M_{f_{z}})\leq\sum_{f_{x}\in F_{x},f_{y}\in F_{y}}\rank(M_{f_{x},f_{y},f_{z}})
.
All in all, this shows that

\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq\sum_{f_{z}\in F_{z}}\rank(M_{f_{z}})\leq\sum_{f\in F}T(\varphi_{f},p-1)=T(\varphi,p),

which concludes the proof.
∎

D.2
Proofs of Theorems
14
and
1

For the proof of
Theorem

14
, we use the following result.

Theorem 39

(Uniform quasi-wideness for tuples
[
31
, Theorem 2.9]
)
.

Let
p,r\in\mathbb{N}
,
and let
\mathcal{C}
be a class of graphs that do not include
K_{p}
as a depth-
18r
minor.
For every
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
there is a number
s
and a polynomial
N\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

computable from
p,r
, and
d
with the following property.

For every
G\in\mathcal{C}
, every
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
and every set
X\subseteq(V(G))^{d}
with
\left\lvert X\right\rvert\geq N(m)
,
there are sets
S\subseteq V(G)
and
Y\subseteq X

with
\left\lvert S\right\rvert\leq s
and
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert\geq m

such that all distinct
\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\in Y

are
r
-separated by
S
in
G
.

We can now prove
Theorem

14
.

See
14

Proof.

Let
r\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N}
and
T\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

be the functions from
Lemma

15
.
We set
g\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N},\xi\mapsto 18r(\xi)
.

Let
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
be a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
counting term,
let
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
and let
\mathcal{C}
be the class of graphs excluding
K_{p}
as a depth-
g(t)
minor.
Let
d\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert
,
and let
s\in\mathbb{N}
be the number and
N\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
be the polynomial
computed from
r(t)
,
p
, and
d
using
Theorem

39
.
Moreover, let
L\coloneqq f(t,p)\coloneqq N(2T(t,s)+6)
.
Since
N
and
s
are computable from
\varphi
and
p
,
we have that
f
is a computable function.

Towards a contradiction, suppose there is a signature
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(t)
,
a
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
,
and tuples
\bar{v}_{1},\dots,\bar{v}_{L}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}

and
\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}

that form a
t
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
,
that is, there exists a function
g\colon\{\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}\}\to\mathbb{Z}

such that
\llbracket t(\bar{v}_{i},\bar{w}_{j})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=g(\bar{w}_{j})
if and only if
i\leq j
.
In particular, the tuples
\bar{v}_{1},\dots,\bar{v}_{L}
are pairwise distinct,
and the same holds for the tuples
\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}
.
Let
X\coloneqq\{\bar{v}_{i}\bar{w}_{i}:i\in[L]\}\subseteq A^{d}
.
By
Theorem

39
,
for
m\coloneqq 2T(t,s)+6
, since
\left\lvert X\right\rvert=L\geq N(m)
,
there are sets
S\subseteq A
and
Y\subseteq X

with
\left\lvert S\right\rvert\leq s
and
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert\geq m
such that all distinct
\bar{u},\bar{u}^{\prime}\in Y

are
r(t)
-separated by
S
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Let
Y^{\prime}\subseteq Y
with
\left\lvert Y^{\prime}\right\rvert=m
,
and let
I\coloneqq\{i\in[L]:\bar{v}_{i}\bar{w}_{i}\in Y^{\prime}\}
.
Let
I_{1},I_{2}
be an alternating partition of
I
, that is,
for all successive
i,j\in I_{1}
, there is exactly one
k\in I_{2}
with
i<k<j
,
and the same holds the other way round.
Note that
\left\lvert I\right\rvert=\left\lvert Y^{\prime}\right\rvert=m
,
and hence
\left\lvert I_{1}\right\rvert=\left\lvert I_{2}\right\rvert=m/2=T(t,s)+3
.
Let
V\subseteq A
be the set of vertices
appearing in a tuple
\bar{v}_{i}\bar{w}_{i}
with
i\in I_{1}
,
and let
W\subseteq A
be the set of vertices
appearing in a tuple
\bar{v}_{i}\bar{w}_{i}
with
i\in I_{2}
,
Since all distinct
\bar{u},\bar{u}^{\prime}\in Y
are
r(t)
-separated by
S
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
,
it also holds that
V
and
W
are
r(t)
-separated by
S
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Now we can apply
Lemma

15
to
V
and
W
,
which shows that
\rank\bigl(M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr)\leq T(t,s)=\left\lvert I_{1}\right\rvert-3
.

On the other hand, we have that
(\bar{v}_{i})_{i\in I_{1}}
,
(\bar{w}_{i})_{i\in I_{2}}

forms a
t
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
via the function

g^{\prime}\colon\{\bar{w}_{j}:j\in I_{2}\}\to\mathbb{Z},\bar{w}\mapsto g(\bar{w})
.
Let
M_{g^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{V^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\times W^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}}

with
(M_{g^{\prime}})_{\bar{v},\bar{w}_{j}}\coloneqq-g^{\prime}(\bar{w}_{j})
for all
\bar{v}\in V^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
and
j\in I_{2}
,
and let
(M_{g^{\prime}})_{\bar{v},\bar{w}_{j}}\coloneqq 0
for all
\bar{v}\in V^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
and
j\not\in I_{2}
.
Furthermore, let
M^{\prime}\coloneqq M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)+M_{g^{\prime}}
.
Then, for all
i\in I_{1}
and
j\in I_{2}
,
we have
M^{\prime}_{\bar{v}_{i},\bar{w}_{j}}=0
if and only if
i\leq j
.
This shows that
M^{\prime}
has rank at least
\left\lvert I_{1}\right\rvert-1
,
and
M_{g^{\prime}}
has rank
1
,
so
M^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)
has rank at least
\left\lvert I_{1}\right\rvert-2
.
This is a contradiction.

Thus, this shows that there is no
t
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
of size at least
L=f(t,p)
,
so the ladder index of
t
in
\mathcal{A}
is at most
f(t,p)
.
∎

See
1

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nowhere dense graph class.
By
Definition

2
,
there is a function
p\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
such that for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
G\in\mathcal{C}
,
it holds that
G
does not contain
K_{p(r)}
as a depth-
r
minor.

Let
f^{\prime}\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
and
g\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N}

be the computable functions from
Theorem

14
.
We set
f\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N},\xi\mapsto f^{\prime}\bigl(\xi,p(g(\xi))\bigr)
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is effectively nowhere dense, then
p
is computable,
so
f
is computable as a composition of computable functions.

Let
t
be a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
counting term
and let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\sigma
-structure for some
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(t)

such that
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
.
Then
G_{\mathcal{A}}
does not contain
K_{p(g(t))}
as a depth-
g(t)
minor.
Hence, by
Theorem

14
, the ladder index of
t
in
\mathcal{A}

is at most
f^{\prime}\bigl(t,p(g(t))\bigr)=f(t)
.

Let
d\coloneqq f(t)
.
Then there is no
t
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
of length
d
.
Thus, for all
\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{d}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}

and all functions
g\colon\{\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{d}\}\to\mathbb{Z}
,
there are no
\bar{v}_{1},\dots,\bar{v}_{d}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}

such that
\llbracket t(\bar{v}_{i},\bar{w}_{j})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=g(\bar{w}_{j})
if and only if
i\leq j
.
Hence, for
W\coloneqq\{\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{d}\}
,
and every function
g\colon W\to\mathbb{Z}
,
there is an index
i\in[d]

such that there is no function
h\in\textup{S}^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/A)

with
h(\bar{w})=g(\bar{w})
if
\bar{w}\in\{\bar{w}_{i},\dots,\bar{w}_{d}\}

and
h(\bar{w})\neq g(\bar{w})
if
\bar{w}\in W\setminus\{\bar{w}_{i},\dots,\bar{w}_{d}\}
.
Since
g
is arbitrary, this shows that
W
is not graph shattered by
\textup{S}^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/A)
.
Furthermore, since
W
was chosen arbitrarily,
there is no set of size
d
that is graph shattered by
\textup{S}^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/A)
,
so the graph dimension of
t
in
\mathcal{A}
is less than
d=f(t)
.
∎

D.3
Proof of Corollary
16

For a formula
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
and
L\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, a
\varphi
-ladder
of length
L

in a structure
\mathcal{A}
is a sequence of tuples
\bar{v}_{1},\dots,\bar{v}_{L},\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}

such that
\bar{v}_{i}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
and
\bar{w}_{i}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}
for all
i\in[L]

and, for all
i,j\in[L]
, it holds that
\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v}_{i},\bar{w}_{j}]

if and only if
i\leq j
.
The smallest
L
for which there is no
\varphi
-ladder of length
L
in
\mathcal{A}

is called the
ladder index of
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
.

See
16

Corollary

16
is a direct consequence of
Theorems

1
and
14

via the following result.

Lemma 40
.

For every
\mathsf{cgFOC}
formula
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
,
every
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(\varphi)
,
and for the
\mathsf{cgFOC}
counting term
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\#{()}.{\varphi}
,
the VC dimension of
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}

is equal to the graph dimension of
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
,
and the ladder index of
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}

is at most the ladder index of
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

For a set
X\subseteq A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}
,
we let
h_{X}\colon A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}\to\mathbb{Z}
be the indicator function for
X

with
h_{X}(x)\coloneqq 1
if
x\in X
, and
h_{X}(x)\coloneqq 0
otherwise.
We set

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/A)

\displaystyle=\{\tp^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}/A):\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{\{\bar{w}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}:\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v},\bar{w}]\}:\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\bigr\}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\coloneqq\textup{S}^{t}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/A)

\displaystyle=\{\tp^{t}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}/A):\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}\to\mathbb{Z},\bar{w}\mapsto\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}:\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{A^{\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert}\to\mathbb{Z},\bar{w}\mapsto\llbracket\varphi(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}:\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\bigr\}.

By definition, the VC dimension of
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
is the VC dimension of
\mathcal{H}
,
and the graph dimension of
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
is the graph dimension of
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\{h_{X}:X\in\mathcal{H}\}
.
Hence, by
Lemma

13
,
the VC dimension of
\mathcal{H}
is equal to the graph dimension of
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
,
so the VC dimension of
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}

is equal to the graph dimension of
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
.

Now let
\bar{v}_{1},\dots,\bar{v}_{L},\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}
be a
\varphi
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
.
Then, for all
i,j\in[L]
, it holds that
\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v}_{i},\bar{w}_{j}]

if and only if
i\leq j
.
Hence, for
g\colon\{\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}\}\to\mathbb{Z}

with
g(\bar{w}_{j})\coloneqq 1
for all
j\in[L]
,
we have
\llbracket t(\bar{v}_{i},\bar{w}_{j})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=g(\bar{w}_{j})
if and only if
i\leq j
.
This shows that
\bar{v}_{1},\dots,\bar{v}_{L},\bar{w}_{1},\dots,\bar{w}_{L}
is also a
t
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
.
Since this holds for every
\varphi
-ladder in
\mathcal{A}
,
the ladder index of
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}

is at most the ladder index of
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
.
∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
17

See
17

Our proof will rely on the following stronger version of
Lemma

15
for formulas:

Corollary 41
.

There are computable functions
T\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

and
r\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N}
such that,
for every
\mathsf{cgFOC}
formula
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
,
every
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
every
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
for some
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(\varphi)
,
and all
V,W\subseteq A
that are
r(\varphi)
-separated in
G_{\mathcal{A}}

by a set of size at most
m
,
we have
\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(V/W)\bigr\rvert\leq T(\varphi,m)
.

Corollary

41
follows immediately from the statement of
Lemma

15

for the counting term
t\coloneqq\#{()}.{\varphi}
,
using the following fact on Boolean matrices over the real numbers
\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 42
.

Let
M\in\{0,1\}^{m\times n}
with
\rank(M)\eqqcolon c
be a Boolean matrix
with
m
pairwise distinct rows. Then
m\leq 2^{c}
.

Proof.

For sets
S\subseteq[m]
and
T\subseteq[n]
,
let
M_{S\times T}
be the submatrix of
M
with entries
whose coordinates are in
S\times T
.

{claim*}

There are sets
S\subseteq[m]
and
T\subseteq[n]

of size
\left\lvert S\right\rvert=\left\lvert T\right\rvert=c
with
\rank(M_{S\times T})=c
.

{claimproof}

Let
S^{\prime}\subseteq[m]
be a set of
\left\lvert S^{\prime}\right\rvert<c
row indices
so that all rows in
S^{\prime}
are linearly independent.
Since
\left\lvert S^{\prime}\right\rvert<c
, there is an index
i\in[m]\setminus S

so that all rows in
M_{(S^{\prime}\cup\{i\})\times[n]}
are linearly independent.
Therefore, we can inductively expand
S^{\prime}
to a set
S
of size exactly
c

that satisfies
\rank(M_{S\times[n]})=c
.
By applying the same reasoning to the columns of
M_{S\times[n]}
,
we obtain a set
T\subseteq[n]
with
\rank(M_{S\times T})=c
.

Let
S\subseteq[m]
and
T\subseteq[n]

of size
\left\lvert S\right\rvert=\left\lvert T\right\rvert=c
with
\rank(M_{S\times T})=c
.
The matrix
M_{[m]\times T}
contains at most
2^{c}
distinct rows.
We show that it cannot contain two identical rows, which implies that
m\leq 2^{c}
.

Since
M_{S\times T}
has full rank, the equation system
x\cdot M_{S\times T}=b

has a unique solution for every
b\in\{0,1\}^{c}
.
Therefore, the system
x^{\prime}\cdot M_{S\times[n]}=b^{\prime}
has at most one solution

x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{c}
for every
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
.
This implies that there can be no two rows in
M
that agree on
T

but differ on
[n]\setminus T
, since every row
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
of
M

can be expressed as
x^{\prime}\cdot M_{S\times[n]}
for some
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{c}

due to
M_{S\times[n]}
having the same rank as the entire matrix
M
.
∎

In the remainder of this section, using
Corollary

41
,
we prove the following theorem, which directly implies
Theorem

17
.

Theorem 43
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nowhere dense graph class,
and let
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
be a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
formula.
For every
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
c\in\mathbb{N}

such that for every
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(\varphi)

and
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}

and every non-empty
W\subseteq A
,
we have
\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W)\bigr\rvert\leq c\cdot\left\lvert W\right\rvert^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert+\varepsilon}
.

For the proof of
Theorem

43
,
we rely on the following lemma on the neighbourhood complexity
in nowhere dense graph classes.
Let
G
be a graph, and let
X\subseteq V(G)
.
For vertices
v\in X
and
w\in V(G)
,
a path
P
from
v
to
w
in
G
is called
X
-avoiding

if all vertices on the path except for
v
are not contained in
X
.
For an
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
w\in V(G)
,
the
r
-projection of
w
on
X
, denoted by
M_{r}^{G}(w,X)
,
is the set of all vertices
v\in X
that are connected to
w

by an
X
-avoiding path of length at most
r
.

Lemma 44

(
[
14
, Lemmas 21 and 22]
,
see also
[
31
, Lemma 3.3]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nowhere dense graph class,
There is a function
f_{\textup{cl}}\colon\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\to\mathbb{N}

and a polynomial-time algorithm that,
given a graph
G\in\mathcal{C}
,
X\subseteq V(G)
,

r\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\delta\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
,
computes a set
\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(X)
,
called the
r
-closure of
X
with respect to
\delta
,
with the following properties.

1.

X\subseteq\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(X)\subseteq V(G)
,

2.

\left\lvert\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(X)\right\rvert\leq f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert X\right\rvert^{1+\delta}
, and

3.

\left\lvert M_{r}^{G}\bigl(u,\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(X)\bigr)\right\rvert\leq f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert X\right\rvert^{\delta}

for all
u\in V(G)\setminus\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(X)
.

Moreover, for all
X\subseteq V(G)
, it holds that

4.

\left\lvert\bigl\{M_{r}^{G}(u,X):u\in V(G)\bigr\}\right\rvert\leq f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert X\right\rvert^{1+\delta}
.

Proof of
Theorem

43
.

The proof is similar to the proof of the analogous result
for graphs in
[
31
]
,
using
Corollary

41
instead of the corresponding result for graphs.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nowhere dense graph class,
let
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})
be a
\mathsf{cgFOC}
formula,
and let
\varepsilon>0
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
\varepsilon\leq 1
.
Let
k\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert
,

\ell\coloneqq\left\lvert\bar{y}\right\rvert
,
let
r\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N}
and
T\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

be the functions from
Corollary

41
,
let
p\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
be the function from
Definition

2
for
\mathcal{C}
,
and let
r\coloneqq r(\varphi)
and
p\coloneqq p(36r)
.
It holds that no graph
G\in\mathcal{C}
contains
K_{p}
as a depth-
36r
minor.

By
Theorem

39
,
there is a number
s\in\mathbb{N}
and a polynomial
N\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

such that, for every graph
G\in\mathcal{C}
, every
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
and every set
X\subseteq(V(G))^{k}
with
\left\lvert X\right\rvert\geq N(m)
,
there are sets
S\subseteq V(G)
and
Y\subseteq X

with
\left\lvert S\right\rvert\leq s
and
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert\geq m

such that all distinct
\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\in Y

are
2r
-separated by
S
in
G
.
Let
d
be the degree of
N
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\sigma
-structure with
\sigma\supseteq\sigma(\varphi)

and
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
,
and let
W\subseteq A
be a non-empty set.
We set
\delta\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon}{4k+4d}
,
and we let
W^{\prime}\coloneqq\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(W)

be the
r
-closure of
W
with respect to
\delta
,
obtained via
Lemma

44
applied to
G_{\mathcal{A}}
.
We shall prove that

\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W^{\prime})\bigr\rvert\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{k+\varepsilon^{\prime}}\bigr)\text{ for }\varepsilon^{\prime}\coloneqq\varepsilon/2>0,

(
\star
)

where
\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(\cdot)
omits factors depending only on
\varepsilon
and
\varphi
.
Since
W\subseteq W^{\prime}
, we have
\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W)\bigr\rvert\leq\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W^{\prime})\bigr\rvert
.
Moreover, by
Lemma

44
,
we have
\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert=\left\lvert\textup{cl}_{r,\delta}(W)\right\rvert\leq f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert W\right\rvert^{1+\delta}
,
and we have

(1+\delta)(k+\varepsilon^{\prime})=(1+\delta)(k+\varepsilon/2)\leq k+\varepsilon

by the choice of
\delta
,
so

\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W)\bigr\rvert\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\Bigl(\bigl(f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert W\right\rvert^{1+\delta}\bigr)^{k+\varepsilon^{\prime}}\Bigr)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W\right\rvert^{k+\varepsilon}\bigr),

which is the statement of
Theorem

43
.

It remains to prove (

⋆

‣
D.4
).
Recall that

\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W^{\prime})=\bigl\{\tp^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}/W^{\prime}):\bar{v}\in A^{k}\bigr\}
.
We partition the tuples

\bar{v}=(v_{1},\dots,v_{k})\in A^{k}

based on their projection
M_{r}^{G_{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{v},W^{\prime})\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}M_{r}^{G_{\mathcal{A}}}(v_{i},W^{\prime})

into sets
V_{1},\dots,V_{t}
.
That is, two tuples
\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\in A^{k}

are contained in the same set
V_{j}
for some
j\in[t]
if and only if

M_{r}^{G_{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{v},W^{\prime})=M_{r}^{G_{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{v}^{\prime},W^{\prime})
.
By
Item

4
of
Lemma

44
,
there are at most
f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{1+\delta}

different projections of vertices in
A=V(G_{\mathcal{A}})
on
W^{\prime}
, so we have

t\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{(1+\delta)k}\bigr)
.
Hence, to prove (

⋆

‣
D.4
),
it suffices to show that

\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\tp^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}/W^{\prime}):\bar{v}\in V_{j}\bigr\}\bigr\rvert\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\bigr),

(
\star\star
)

for
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\varepsilon^{\prime}-k\delta>0

and all
j\in[t]
, implying

\bigl\lvert\mkern 1.0mu\textup{S}^{\varphi}_{\!\mathcal{A}}(A/W^{\prime})\bigr\rvert\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{(1+\delta)k}\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{\varepsilon^{\prime}-k\delta}\bigr)=\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{k+\varepsilon^{\prime}}\bigr)
.

Let
j\in[t]
,
and let
X\coloneqq M_{r}^{G_{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{v},W^{\prime})
be the
r
-projection of
\bar{v}
on
W^{\prime}

for any (and, due to the definition of
V_{j}
, for all)
\bar{v}\in V_{j}
.
By
Item

3
of
Lemma

44
,
we have
\left\lvert X\right\rvert\leq k\cdot f_{\textup{cl}}(r,\delta)\cdot\left\lvert W\right\rvert^{\delta}\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}\bigl(\left\lvert W\right\rvert^{\delta}\bigr)
.

Let
V^{\prime}_{j}
be a maximal subset of
V_{j}
such that
all pairwise distinct tuples
\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}
from
V^{\prime}_{j}

have different types

\tp^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}/W^{\prime})\neq\tp^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}^{\prime}/W^{\prime})
.
Note that

\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\tp^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}/W^{\prime}):\bar{v}\in V_{j}\bigr\}\bigr\rvert=\bigl\lvert V^{\prime}_{j}\bigr\rvert
.
Now let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be the maximum number with

\bigl\lvert V^{\prime}_{j}\bigr\rvert\geq N(m)
.
Then
\bigl\lvert V^{\prime}_{j}\bigr\rvert<N(m+1)\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(m^{d})
.

By
Theorem

39
,
as described above,
there are sets
S\subseteq A=V(G_{\mathcal{A}})
and
Y\subseteq V^{\prime}_{j}

with
\left\lvert S\right\rvert\leq s
and
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert\geq m

such that all distinct
\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\in Y

are
2r
-separated by
S
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
.

We partition
Y
into two sets
Y_{1}\uplus Y_{2}
,
where
Y_{1}
contains all tuples that are
r
-separated by
S
from
W^{\prime}
,
and
Y_{2}
contains the remaining tuples.
By
Corollary

41
, since all tuples in
Y_{1}
are
r
-separated by
S
from
W^{\prime}
,
and all tuples in
Y_{1}
have distinct types,
we know that
\left\lvert Y_{1}\right\rvert\leq T(\varphi,s)\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(1)
.
Moreover, for every tuple
\bar{v}\in Y_{2}
,
there is a vertex
w\in W^{\prime}
such that
\bar{v}
and
w

are not
r
-separated by
S
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Note that we can choose
w
to be contained in
X
.
Furthermore, since all tuples in
Y_{2}
are mutually
2r
-separated by
S
in
G_{\mathcal{A}}
,
we know that for two distinct tuples
\bar{v},\bar{v}^{\prime}\in Y_{2}
,
the vertices in
X
connected to them by paths of length at most
r

avoiding
S
must also be distinct.
This shows that
\left\lvert Y_{2}\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert X\right\rvert
.
Combined, we obtain that
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\delta}(\left\lvert X\right\rvert)
.
Furthermore, since
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert\geq m
,
we have

\left\lvert V^{\prime}_{j}\right\rvert\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(m^{d})\ \subseteq\ \mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(\left\lvert Y\right\rvert^{d})\ \subseteq\ \mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(\left\lvert X\right\rvert^{d})\ \subseteq\ \mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{d\delta})\ \subseteq\ \mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon,\varphi}(\left\lvert W^{\prime}\right\rvert^{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}),

where the last inclusion holds because

d\delta\leq\varepsilon/4\leq\varepsilon/2-k\delta=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}

by the choice of
\delta
.
This proves (

⋆
⁣
⋆

‣
D.4
), which,
as discussed above, implies the statement of
Theorem

43
.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs Omitted in Section
5

E.1
Proof of Theorem
18

Lemma 45

(Sauer–Shelah–Perles
[
32
,
37
,
40
]
)
.

Let
X
be a set, and let
\mathcal{H}\subseteq 2^{X}
have finite VC dimension
d\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, for every finite subset
Y\subseteq X
, it holds that

\left\lvert\bigl\{Y\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}\bigr\}\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=0}^{d}\binom{\left\lvert Y\right\rvert}{i}
.

This implies the following.

Lemma 46
.

Let
X
be a set, and let
\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}\subseteq 2^{X}

such that
\mathcal{H}_{1}
has finite VC dimension
d_{1}\in\mathbb{N}

and
\mathcal{H}_{2}
has finite VC dimension
d_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
.
It holds that
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}_{2}
has VC dimension at most
d_{1}+d_{2}+1
.

Proof.

Let
X^{\prime}\subseteq X
be a finite set of size
\left\lvert X^{\prime}\right\rvert=d_{1}+d_{2}+2
.
By
Lemma

45
, it holds that

\left\lvert\bigl\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}_{i}\bigr\}\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=0}^{d_{j}}\binom{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}{i}

for
j\in[2]
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigl\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigl\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\bigr\}\cup\bigl\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bigl\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\bigr\}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bigl\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}_{2}\bigr\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{d_{1}}\binom{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}{j}+\sum_{j=0}^{d_{2}}\binom{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}{j}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{d_{1}}\binom{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}{j}+\sum_{j=0}^{d_{2}}\binom{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}{d_{1}+d_{2}+2-j}

\displaystyle<~

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}\binom{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}{j}=2^{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}.

This shows that
X^{\prime}
is not shattered by
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}_{2}
.
Since
X^{\prime}
was chosen arbitrarily,
this implies that the VC dimension of
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}_{2}
is at most
d_{1}+d_{2}+1
.
∎

We now prove an analogous result on the graph dimension.

Lemma 47
.

Let
X
be a set, and let
\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}
be two sets of functions

h\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{1}
has finite graph dimension
d_{1}\in\mathbb{N}

and
\mathcal{H}_{2}
has finite graph dimension
d_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
.
It holds that
\mathcal{H}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}_{2}
has graph dimension at most
d_{1}+d_{2}+1
.

Proof.

We prove the statement based on the analogous statement for the VC dimension
in
Lemma

46
.
Let
X^{\prime}\subseteq X
be a finite set of size
\left\lvert X^{\prime}\right\rvert=d_{1}+d_{2}+2
,
and let
g\colon X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{Z}
.
We show that for every such
X^{\prime}
and
g
,
there is a set
Y\subseteq X^{\prime}
such that there is no function

h\in\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}_{2}
with

h(x)=g(x)
for all
x\in Y

and
h(x)\neq g(x)
for all
x\in X^{\prime}\setminus Y
.
This shows that there is no set
X^{\prime}
of size
d_{1}+d_{2}+2

that is graph shattered by
\mathcal{H}
,
so the graph dimension of
\mathcal{H}
is at most
d_{1}+d_{2}+1
.

Let
g^{\prime}\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function with
g^{\prime}(x)=g(x)
for all
x\in X^{\prime}
,
let
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq\bigl\{\{x\in X:h(x)=g^{\prime}(x)\}:h\in\mathcal{H}_{i}\bigr\}\subseteq 2^{X}

for
i\in[2]
,
and let
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{1}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{2}=\bigl\{\{x\in X:h(x)=g^{\prime}(x)\}:h\in\mathcal{H}\bigr\}
.

Since
\mathcal{H}_{1}
has graph dimension
d_{1}
,
for every set
X^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq X
of size
\lvert X^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=d_{1}+1
,
there is no function
g_{1}\colon X^{\prime}_{1}\to\mathbb{Z}
such that
for every set
Y_{1}\subseteq X^{\prime}_{1}
, there is a function
h\in\mathcal{H}_{1}

with
h(x)=g_{1}(x)
for all
x\in Y_{1}

and
h(x)\neq g_{1}(x)
for all
x\in X^{\prime}_{1}\setminus Y_{1}
.
In particular, there is a set
Y_{1}\subseteq X^{\prime}_{1}

such that there is no function
h\in\mathcal{H}_{1}

with
h(x)=g^{\prime}(x)
for all
x\in Y_{1}

and
h(x)\neq g^{\prime}(x)
for all
x\in X^{\prime}_{1}\setminus Y_{1}
.
This shows that
Y_{1}\not\in\{X^{\prime}_{1}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{1}\}
,
so
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{1}
does not shatter
X^{\prime}_{1}
.
Since this holds for every set
X^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq X
of size
\lvert X^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=d_{1}+1
,
this implies that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{1}
has VC dimension at most
d_{1}
.
Analogously, it holds that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{2}
has VC dimension at most
d_{2}
.

Using
Lemma

46
, we obtain that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

has VC dimension at most
d_{1}+d_{2}+1
,
so
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
does not shatter
X^{\prime}
.
Thus, there is a set
Y\subseteq X^{\prime}

with
Y\not\in\{X^{\prime}\cap H:H\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\}
,
so there is no function
h\in\mathcal{H}
with

h(x)=g^{\prime}(x)=g(x)
for all
x\in Y

and
h(x)\neq g^{\prime}(x)=g(x)
for all
x\in X^{\prime}\setminus Y
.
As described above, since
X^{\prime}
and
g
were chosen arbitrarily,
this shows that
\mathcal{H}
has graph dimension at most
d_{1}+d_{2}+1
.
∎

For a set
X
, a probability distribution
\mathcal{D}
on
X\times\{0,1\}
,
a sequence
S=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

over
X\times\{0,1\}
,
and a subset
X^{\prime}\subseteq X
of
X
,
we let
\err_{\mathcal{D}}(X^{\prime})\coloneqq\Pr_{(x,\lambda)\sim\mathcal{D}}\bigl(x\in X^{\prime}\iff\lambda=0\bigr)

and
\err_{S}(X^{\prime})\coloneqq\frac{1}{s}\cdot\bigl\lvert\{i\in[s]:x_{i}\in X^{\prime}\iff\lambda_{i}=0\}\bigr\rvert
.

Lemma 48

(Uniform Convergence,

[
36
, Sections 6.4 and 6.5]
)
.

There is a computable function
s_{\textup{{uc}}}
with the following property.
Let
X
be a set, let
\mathcal{H}\subseteq 2^{X}
be of finite VC dimension
c\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathcal{D}
be a probability distribution on
X\times\{0,1\}
,
and let
\varepsilon,\delta\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
.
For every sequence
S=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

of length
s\geq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
drawn
i.i.d.
from
\mathcal{D}
,
where
(x_{i},\lambda_{i})\in X\times\{0,1\}
,
with probability at least
1-\delta
,
it holds that
\bigl\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}}(h)-\err_{S}(h)\bigr\rvert\leq\varepsilon
for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
.
Moreover,
s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
is monotonically increasing in
c

and monotonically decreasing in
\varepsilon
and
\delta
.

For a set
X
, a probability distribution
\mathcal{D}
on
X\times\mathbb{Z}
,
a sequence
S=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

over
X\times\mathbb{Z}
,
and a function
h\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
,
we let
\err_{\mathcal{D}}(h)=\Pr_{(x,\lambda)\sim\mathcal{D}}\bigl(h(x)\neq\lambda\bigr)

and
\err_{S}(h)=\frac{1}{s}\cdot\bigl\lvert\{i\in[s]:h(x_{i})\neq\lambda_{i}\}\bigr\rvert
.
We prove the following analogue of
Lemma

48
for the graph dimension.

Lemma 49
.

There is a computable function
s_{\textup{{uc}}}
with the following property.
Let
X
be a set, let
\mathcal{H}
be a set of functions
h\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}

such that
\mathcal{H}
has finite graph dimension
c\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathcal{D}
be a probability distribution on
X\times\mathbb{Z}
,
and let
\varepsilon,\delta\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
.
For every sequence
S=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})
over
X\times\mathbb{Z}

of length
s\geq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
drawn
i.i.d.
from
\mathcal{D}
,
with probability at least
1-\delta
,
it holds that
\bigl\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}}(h)-\err_{S}(h)\bigr\rvert\leq\varepsilon
for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
.
Moreover,
s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
is monotonically increasing in
c

and monotonically decreasing in
\varepsilon
and
\delta
.

Proof.

Let
s_{\textup{{uc}}}
be the computable function from
Lemma

48
,
let
s\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
with
s\geq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
,
and let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of all sequences
S=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

over
X\times\mathbb{Z}
such that

\left\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}}(h)-\err_{S}(h)\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon
for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
.
We show that
\mathcal{D}^{s}(\mathcal{S})\geq 1-\delta
,
which proves
Lemma

49
.
1
1

1

\mathcal{D}^{s}
is the probability distribution on
(X\times\mathbb{Z})^{s}

where we draw
s
times
i.i.d.
from
\mathcal{D}
.

We set
X^{\prime}\coloneqq X\times\mathbb{Z}
.
For a function
h\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
,
let
X^{\prime}_{h}\coloneqq\bigl\{\bigl(x,h(x)\bigr):x\in X\bigr\}\subset X^{\prime}
,
and set
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{X^{\prime}_{h}:h\in\mathcal{H}\}\subseteq 2^{X^{\prime}}
.

{claim*}

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
has VC dimension at most
c
.

{claimproof}

Let
Y^{\prime}\subseteq X^{\prime}
be a set of size
\left\lvert Y^{\prime}\right\rvert=c+1
.
If there are
(x,i_{1}),(x,i_{2})\in Y^{\prime}
for some
x\in X
and
i_{1},i_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}

with
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
,
then there is no
X^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
with
Y^{\prime}\subseteq X^{\prime}_{h}
,
since either
i_{1}\neq h(x)
or
i_{2}\neq h(x)
,
so
Y^{\prime}\not\in\{Y^{\prime}\cap X^{\prime}_{h}:X^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\}
.
This shows that
Y^{\prime}
is not shattered by
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

In the other case, for every
x\in X
,
there is at most one
i\in\mathbb{Z}
with
(x,i)\in Y^{\prime}
.
Let
Y
be the set of all
x\in X
such that
(x,i)\in Y^{\prime}
for some
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then
\left\lvert Y\right\rvert=\left\lvert Y^{\prime}\right\rvert=c+1
.
Let
g\colon Y\to\mathbb{Z}
be the function with
g(x)\coloneqq i

for every
(x,i)\in Y^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathcal{H}
has graph dimension
c
,
we know that
Y
is not graph shattered by
\mathcal{H}
.
Hence, there exists a set
A\subseteq Y
such that for every function
h\in\mathcal{H}
,
we have
g(x)\neq h(x)
for some
x\in A

or
g(x)=h(x)
for some
x\in Y\setminus A
.
Let
A^{\prime}\subseteq Y^{\prime}
be the set
that contains exactly those
(x,i)\in Y^{\prime}
with
x\in A
.
Then, for every
X^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
,
we have
g(x)\neq h(x)
for some
x\in A
,
which implies
(x,i)\in A^{\prime}
and
(x,i)\not\in X^{\prime}_{h}
,
or we have
g(x)=h(x)
for some
x\in Y\setminus A
,
which implies
(x,i)\not\in A^{\prime}
and
(x,i)\in X^{\prime}_{h}
.
Thus, for every
X^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
,
it holds that
A^{\prime}\neq X^{\prime}_{h}\cap Y^{\prime}
.
As in the first case, this shows that
Y^{\prime}
is not shattered by
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Hence, in all cases,
Y^{\prime}
is not shattered by
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.
Since
Y^{\prime}
was chosen arbitrarily of size
c+1
,
there is no set of size
c+1
that is shattered by
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
,
implying that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
has VC dimension at most
c
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be the probability distribution on
X^{\prime}\times\{0,1\}

obtained from
\mathcal{D}
by setting
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(A)\coloneqq\mathcal{D}\bigl(\{x\in X^{\prime}:(x,1)\in A\}\bigr)

for every
A\subseteq X^{\prime}\times\{0,1\}
.
Moreover, let
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
be the set of all sequences

S^{\prime}=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

over
X^{\prime}\times\{0,1\}
with

\left\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(h^{\prime})-\err_{S^{\prime}}(h^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon
for all
h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.
Equivalently, for a sequence
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
,
we have that
\bigl\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}_{h})-\err_{S^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}_{h})\bigr\rvert\leq\varepsilon
for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
.
Since
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
has VC dimension at most
c

and
s\geq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
, it holds that

\Pr_{S^{\prime}\sim(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{s}}\bigl(\left\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(h^{\prime})-\err_{S^{\prime}}(h^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon\text{ for all }h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\bigr)\geq 1-\delta

by
Lemma

48
.
Hence,
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{s}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})\geq 1-\delta
.
Let
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
be the set that contains only those sequences

S^{\prime}=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

from
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
with
\lambda_{i}=1
for all
i\in[s]
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, we have

(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{s}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime})=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{s}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
.
Furthermore, for a hypothesis
h\in\mathcal{H}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\err_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}_{h})

\displaystyle=\Pr_{((x,i),\lambda)\sim\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\bigl((x,i)\not\in X^{\prime}_{h}\iff\lambda=1\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Pr_{(x,i)\sim\mathcal{D}}\bigl((x,i)\not\in X^{\prime}_{h}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Pr_{(x,i)\sim\mathcal{D}}\bigl(h(x)\neq i\bigr)

\displaystyle=\err_{\mathcal{D}}(h).

Moreover, for a sequence
S=(x_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(x_{s},\lambda_{s})

over
X\times\mathbb{Z}
and the sequence

S^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigl((x_{1},\lambda_{1}),1\bigr),\dots,\bigl((x_{s},\lambda_{s}),1\bigr)
,
it holds that

\displaystyle\err_{S^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}_{h})

\displaystyle={\textstyle\frac{1}{s}}\cdot\left\lvert\{i\in[s]:(x_{i},\lambda_{i})\not\in X^{\prime}_{h}\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle={\textstyle\frac{1}{s}}\cdot\left\lvert\{i\in[s]:h(x_{i})\neq\lambda_{i}\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\err_{S}(h).

Hence,
\left\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}}(h)-\err_{S}(h)\right\rvert=\bigl\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}_{h})-\err_{S^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}_{h})\bigr\rvert

for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
,
so
S\in\mathcal{S}
if and only if
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
.
This shows that
\mathcal{D}^{s}(\mathcal{S})=(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{s}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime})
.
Thus, all in all, it holds that
\mathcal{D}^{s}(\mathcal{S})\geq 1-\delta
,
which finishes this proof.
∎

In order to prove
Theorem

18
, we first give an ERM algorithm that can then be turned into an agnostic-PAC-learning algorithm.

Proposition 50
.

For every graph class
\mathcal{C}
of locally bounded expansion,
there is a function
f
and an algorithm that does the following.
Given a signature
\sigma
, a
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
,
natural numbers
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell,m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
with
k+\ell\leq m
,
a rational number
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
,
a finite set of integers
I
,
and a sequence
S=(\bar{w}_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bar{w}_{s},\lambda_{s})

over
A^{\ell}\times\mathbb{Z}

for some
s\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
the algorithm runs in time
f(\alpha,\sigma,I,m)\cdot s\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{\max(k,1+\alpha)}
,
and it returns a list consisting of the tuples
\bigl(t,\bar{v},\err_{S}(t,\bar{v})\bigr)

for all
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in T[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
and
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
,
sorted in ascending lexicographic order on
\bigl(\err_{S}(t,\bar{v}),\left\lvert\bar{v}\right\rvert,\left\lvert t\right\rvert\bigr)
.

Proof.

Let
f_{\ref*{thm:answering-enumeration}}
be the function from
Theorem

9
.
Moreover, for every
i\in[0,s]
, let
L_{i}
be an empty list.

For every counting term
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in T[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
,
in ascending lexicographic order on
(\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert,\left\lvert t\right\rvert)
, we do the following.
First, we run the preprocessing from
Theorem

9
on
t
,
\mathcal{A}
, and
\alpha
.
This takes time
f_{\ref*{thm:answering-enumeration}}(t,\sigma(t),\alpha)\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}
.
Next, for every tuple
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
and every
i\in[s]
,
we compute
\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w}_{i})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}
based on the preprocessing above.
This gives us the value

\err_{S}(t,\bar{v})=\frac{1}{s}\cdot\left\lvert\{i\in[s]:\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w}_{i})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}\neq\lambda_{i}\}\right\rvert
,
and we append
\bigl(t,\bar{v},\err_{S}(t,\bar{v})\bigr)
to the list
L_{s\cdot\err_{S}(t,\bar{v})}
.
Over all tuples
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
and all
i\in[s]
, this takes time

f_{\ref*{thm:answering-enumeration}}(t,\sigma(t),\alpha)\cdot s\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\leq f_{\ref*{thm:answering-enumeration}}(t,\sigma(t),\alpha)\cdot s\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{k}
.

In the end, we output the concatenation of the lists
L_{0},L_{1},\dots,L_{s}
.
All in all, since the set
T[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
is computable
from
\sigma
,
m
, and
I
, there is a function
f
such that the described algorithm
runs in time
f(\alpha,\sigma,I,m)\cdot s\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{\max(k,1+\alpha)}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Theorem

18
.

See
18

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a graph class of effectively locally bounded expansion,
and let
f_{\textup{graph}}
be the computable function
from
Theorem

1
for
\mathcal{C}

such that for every signature
\sigma
, every
\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
counting term
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
,

\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
,
the graph dimension of
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
in
\mathcal{A}
is at most
f_{\textup{graph}}(t)
.
Let
s_{\textup{{uc}}}
be the computable function from

49
,
let
c\coloneqq\bigl\lvert T[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]\bigr\rvert+\sum_{t\in T[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]}f_{\textup{graph}}(t)
,

s\coloneqq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
.
Then
s
is computable from
\sigma
,
k
,
\ell
,

,
I
,

\varepsilon
, and
\delta
.

Our algorithm for
Theorem

18
draws a sequence
S

of
s
samples
(\bar{w}_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bar{w}_{s},\lambda_{s})

i.i.d.
from

using the oracle and then runs the algorithm from

on

,

,

,

,

,

, and

.

.

, the graph dimension of
\mathcal{H}

.

,

over the oracle calls to

,

.
