E.2
Proof of Theorem
19

For the proof of
Theorem

19
,
we use the following two results from
[
27
]
.

Theorem 51

(
[
27
, Theorem 3.2]
)
.

For every
d\in\mathbb{N}
and every
\mathsf{FOC}
formula
\varphi(\bar{x})
,
there is an
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma(\varphi)]
formula
\varphi^{\prime}(\bar{x})

that is computable from
d
and
\varphi
such that

\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
are
d
-equivalent and

\varphi^{\prime}(\bar{x})
is a Boolean combination of
\mathsf{FO}[\sigma(\varphi)]
formulas
and
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma(\varphi)]
sentences.

Theorem 52

(
[
27
, Corollary 5.7]
)
.

There are a computable function
f
and an algorithm that does the following.
Given a
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
for some signature
\sigma
,
given
d\in\mathbb{N}
such that
G_{\mathcal{A}}
has degree at most
d
,
and given an
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma]
formula
\varphi(\bar{x})
,
after preprocessing in time
f(\left\lvert\varphi\right\rvert,d)\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert
,
the algorithm enumerates all tuples
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}

such that
\mathcal{A}\models\varphi[\bar{v}]
with
f(\left\lvert\varphi\right\rvert,d)
delay.

We can now prove
Theorem

19
.

See
19

Proof.

Let
g_{\ref*{thm:ladder-index-term}}
and
f_{\ref*{thm:ladder-index-term}}

be the computable functions from
Theorem

14
,
and let
f_{\ref*{thm:fo-enumeration}}
be the computable function
from
Theorem

36
.

Let
\sigma
be a signature, let
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
and let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\sigma
-structure such that
G_{\mathcal{A}}
has degree at most
d
.
Then, for every
r\in\mathbb{N}
and every
v\in A
,
we have
\bigl\lvert N_{r+1}^{\mathcal{A}}(v)\bigr\rvert<(d+1)^{r+2}+1\eqqcolon p(r,d)
,
so
G_{\mathcal{A}}
excludes
K_{p(r,d)}
as a depth-
r
minor.
Hence, by
Theorem

14
,
analogously to the proof of
Theorem

1
,
the graph dimension of a
\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
counting term
t
in
\mathcal{A}
is at most

f_{\textup{graph}}(t,d)\coloneqq f_{\ref*{thm:ladder-index-term}}\bigl(t,p\bigl(g_{\ref*{thm:ladder-index-term}}(t),d\bigr)\bigr)
,
which is computable from
t
and
d
.

Let
T\coloneqq T_{\mathsf{FOC}}[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
,
and let
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in T
.
Then
t
is a polynomial of integers from
I

and of #-terms
u
of the form
\#{\bar{z}}.{\varphi}

for an
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma]
formula
\varphi
.
For every such #-term
u
, we apply
Theorem

51
to
\varphi
and
d
,
and we obtain an
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma]
formula
\varphi^{\prime}

that is a Boolean combination of
\mathsf{FO}[\sigma]
formulas and
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma]
sentences.
Let
\Phi
be the set of all
\mathsf{FO}[\sigma]
formulas obtained from
\varphi^{\prime}

by replacing the
\mathsf{FOC}
sentences by
\top()
or
\bot()
,
let
T_{u}
be the set of all
\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
counting terms
of the form
\#{\bar{z}}.{\psi}
for a formula
\psi\in\Phi
,
and let
T^{\prime}_{t}
be the set of all
\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
counting terms obtained from
t

by replacing every #-term
u
in
t
by a counting term from
T_{u}
.
Then
T^{\prime}_{t}
can be computed from
t
and
d
.
Furthermore, for every
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
of degree at most
d
,
there is a counting term
t^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}_{t}
that is equivalent to
t
on
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
T^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{t\in T}T^{\prime}_{t}
.
Then
T^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
is computable from

\sigma
,
k
,
\ell
,
m
,
I
, and
d
.

Let
s_{\textup{{uc}}}
be the computable function from
Lemma

49
,
let
c\coloneqq\left\lvert T^{\prime}\right\rvert+\sum_{t^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}f_{\textup{graph}}(t^{\prime},d)
,
and
s\coloneqq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
.
Then
s
is computable from
\sigma
,
k
,
\ell
,
m
,
I
,
d
,

\varepsilon
, and
\delta
.

Our algorithm for
Theorem

19
computes
T
and
s
.
Next, it draws a sequence
S=(\bar{w}_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bar{w}_{s},\lambda_{s})

from
\mathcal{D}
using the oracle.

Let
\sigma^{\prime}\coloneqq\sigma\uplus\{R_{i,j}:i\in[s],j\in[\ell]\}

for fresh unary relation symbols
R_{i,j}
,
and let
\mathcal{A}_{S}
be the
\sigma^{\prime}
-expansion of
\mathcal{A}

with
R_{i,j}(\mathcal{A}_{S})\coloneqq\{(\bar{w}_{i})_{j}\}
.

For every counting term
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in T
and every subsequence
S^{\prime}
of
S
,
we define an
\mathsf{FOC}[\sigma^{\prime}]
formula
\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}(\bar{x})

such that, for every tuple
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}
,
we have
\mathcal{A}_{S}\models\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}[\bar{v}]

if and only if
\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=\lambda
for all
(\bar{w},\lambda)\in S^{\prime}

and
\llbracket t(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}\neq\lambda
for all
(\bar{w},\lambda)\in S\setminus S^{\prime}
.
For this, we let

\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}(\bar{x})\coloneqq\forall y_{1}\dots\forall y_{\ell}\Bigl(\mkern-5.0mu\bigwedge_{(\bar{w}_{i},\lambda_{i})\in S^{\prime}}\mkern-5.0mu\psi_{t,i,+}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\land\mkern-18.0mu\bigwedge_{(\bar{w}_{i},\lambda_{i})\in S\setminus S^{\prime}}\mkern-5.0mu\psi_{t,i,-}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\Bigr)

with

\psi_{t,i,+}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\bigl(\bigwedge_{j\in[\ell]}R_{i,j}(y_{j})\bigr)\rightarrow\mathsf{P}_{=}\bigl(t(\bar{x},\bar{y}),\lambda_{i}\bigr)

and

\psi_{t,i,-}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\bigl(\bigwedge_{j\in[\ell]}R_{i,j}(y_{j})\bigr)\rightarrow\neg\mathsf{P}_{=}\bigl(t(\bar{x},\bar{y}),\lambda_{i}\bigr).

It can easily be verified that
\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}
satisfies the properties above,
and there is a bound for the length
\lvert\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}\rvert
of
\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}

that can be computed from
t
,
s
, and
\ell
,
which are computable from
\sigma
,
k
,
\ell
,
m
,
I
,
d
,

\varepsilon
, and
\delta
.

In our algorithm for
Theorem

19
,
for every
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in T
and every subsequence
S^{\prime}
of
S
,
we run the preprocessing from the
\mathsf{FOC}
-enumeration result
Theorem

52

on
\mathcal{A}_{S}
and
\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}

in time
f_{\ref*{thm:foc-enumeration}}\bigl(\lvert\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}\rvert,d\bigr)\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert
.
This finishes the preprocessing of our algorithm,
and there is a computable function
f_{1}

such that the preprocessing takes time at most

f_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m,d)\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert
.

In the enumeration phase, we iterate over all subsequences
S^{\prime}
of
S

with decreasing length, starting with the longest subsequence
S^{\prime}=S
.
We iterate over all counting terms
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in T
,
in ascending lexicographic order on
(\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert,\left\lvert t\right\rvert)
.
For every such counting term,
based on the preprocessing above,
we iterate over all tuples
\bar{v}\in A^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}

that satisfy
\mathcal{A}_{S}\models\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}[\bar{v}]
,
and we output
(t,\bar{v},1-\left\lvert S^{\prime}\right\rvert/\left\lvert S\right\rvert)
for every such tuple.
Because of the preprocessing above, for every counting term
t
and subsequence
S^{\prime}
,
the enumeration has

f_{\ref*{thm:foc-enumeration}}\bigl(\lvert\varphi_{t,S^{\prime}}\rvert,d\bigr)
delay.
Since an upper bound on the number of counting terms
t\in T

and the number of subsequences
S^{\prime}
of
S
can be computed from

\varepsilon
,
\delta
,
\sigma
,
I
,
m
, and
d
,
there is a computable function
f_{2}
such that the enumeration overall
has at most
f_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m,d)
delay.
Setting
f(\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m,d)\coloneqq\max\bigl(f_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m,d),f_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m,d)\bigr)
finishes the proof of the running-time bounds.

Lastly, the set

\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\bigcup_{t\in T}S^{t}_{\mathcal{A}}(A/A)\subseteq\bigcup_{t\in T^{\prime}}S^{t}_{\mathcal{A}}(A/A)

has graph dimension at most
c
by
Lemma

47
.
Thus, analogously to the proof of
Theorem

18
,
the error bounds follow from
Lemma

49
.
∎

E.3
Proof of Theorem
20

For a signature
\sigma
, numbers
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\ell,m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
and a finite set of integers
I\subset\mathbb{Z}
,
let
\Phi[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
be the set of all
\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
formulas

\varphi(x_{1},\dots,x_{k^{\prime}},y_{1},\dots,y_{\ell})

with
k^{\prime}\leq k
,
\left\lvert\varphi\right\rvert\leq m

and such that
\varphi
only uses variables from

\{x_{1},\dots,x_{k},y_{1},\dots,y_{\ell},z_{1},\dots,z_{m}\}

and integers from
I
.
Analogously to the set
T[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
,
the set
\Phi[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]
is finite and computable from

\sigma
,
k
,
\ell
,
m
, and
I
.

See
20

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a graph class of effectively locally bounded expansion,
and let
\sigma
be a signature.
By
Corollary

16
,
there is a computable function
f_{\textup{{vc}}}\colon\mathsf{cgFOC}\to\mathbb{N}
such that
for every
\mathsf{cgFOC}[\sigma]
formula
\varphi

and every
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
,
the VC dimension of
\varphi
in
\mathcal{A}
is a most
f_{\textup{{vc}}}(\varphi)
.
Hence, given
k
,
\ell
,
m
, and
I
,
we can compute
\Phi\coloneqq\Phi[\sigma,k,\ell,m,I]

and
c\coloneqq\left\lvert\Phi\right\rvert+\sum_{\varphi\in\Phi}f_{\textup{{vc}}}(\varphi)
.
Then, for every
\sigma
-structure
\mathcal{A}
with
G_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{C}
,
by
Lemma

46
,
the set
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\coloneqq\bigcup_{\varphi\in\Phi}S^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{A}}(A/A)
has VC dimension at most
c
.

Let
s_{\textup{{uc}}}
be the computable function from
Lemma

48
,
let
s\coloneqq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
,
and let
S=(\bar{w}_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bar{w}_{s},\lambda_{s})

be a sequence drawn
i.i.d.
from
\mathcal{D}
.
Then, by
Lemma

48
,
with probability at least
1-\delta
,
it holds that
\left\lvert\err_{\mathcal{D}}(\varphi,\bar{v})-\err_{S}(\varphi,\bar{v})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon

for every formula
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\Phi
and every tuple
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
,
where

\err_{S}(\varphi,\bar{v})\coloneqq{\textstyle\frac{1}{s}}\cdot\left\lvert\{i\in[s]:\llbracket\varphi(\bar{v},\bar{w}_{i})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}\neq\lambda_{i}\}\right\rvert.

Let
\sigma^{\prime}\coloneqq\sigma\uplus\{R_{i,j}:i\in[s],j\in[\ell]\}

for fresh unary relation symbols
R_{i,j}
.
We let
\mathcal{A}_{S}
be the
\sigma^{\prime}
-expansion of
\mathcal{A}

with
R_{i,j}(\mathcal{A}_{S})\coloneqq\{(\bar{w}_{i})_{j}\}
.
For every subsequence
S^{\prime}
of
S
and every formula
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\Phi
, we set

\displaystyle\varphi_{S^{\prime}}(\bar{x})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\forall y_{1}\dots\forall y_{\ell}\Bigl(\mkern-5.0mu\bigwedge_{(\bar{w}_{i},\lambda_{i})\in S^{\prime}}\mkern-5.0mu\psi_{i,+}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\land\mkern-25.0mu\bigwedge_{(\bar{w}_{i},\lambda_{i})\in S\setminus S^{\prime}}\mkern-5.0mu\psi_{i,-}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\Bigr)

with

\displaystyle\psi_{i,+}(\bar{x},\bar{y})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigl(\bigwedge_{j\in[\ell]}R_{i,j}(y_{j})\bigr)\rightarrow\varphi_{\lambda_{i}}(\bar{x},\bar{y}),

\displaystyle\psi_{i,-}(\bar{x},\bar{y})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigl(\bigwedge_{j\in[\ell]}R_{i,j}(y_{j})\bigr)\rightarrow\varphi_{1-\lambda_{i}}(\bar{x},\bar{y}),

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(\bar{x},\bar{y})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\neg\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y}),\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{1}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\coloneqq\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y}).

Then, for every
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
, it holds that

\mathcal{A}_{S}\models\varphi_{S^{\prime}}[\bar{v}]

if and only if
\llbracket\varphi(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}=\lambda
for all
(\bar{w},\lambda)
in
S^{\prime}

and
\llbracket\varphi(\bar{v},\bar{w})\rrbracket^{\mathcal{A}}\neq\lambda
for all
(\bar{w},\lambda)
in
S\setminus S^{\prime}
.
Hence, for all
\bar{v}\in A^{k}
with
\mathcal{A}_{S}\models\varphi_{S^{\prime}}[\bar{v}]
,
we have
\err_{S}(\varphi,\bar{v})=1-\left\lvert S^{\prime}\right\rvert/\left\lvert S\right\rvert
.

In the algorithm for
Theorem

20
,
the preprocessing works as follows.
First, we compute
\Phi
,
c
, and
s\coloneqq s_{\textup{{uc}}}(c,\varepsilon,\delta)
as described above.
Next, we draw a sequence
S=(\bar{w}_{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bar{w}_{s},\lambda_{s})

of length
s
from
\mathcal{D}
using the oracle,
and we compute the structure
\mathcal{A}_{S}
.
Finally, for every subsequence
S^{\prime}
of
S
, we run the preprocessing
from
Theorem

9
on
\mathcal{A}_{S}
and
\varphi_{S^{\prime}}
.
This allows us to enumerate all tuples
\bar{v}
that satisfy
\mathcal{A}_{S}\models\varphi_{S^{\prime}}[\bar{v}]

with constant delay.
Since
s
and
\Phi
only depend on
\sigma
,
k
,
\ell
,
m
,
I
,

\varepsilon
, and
\delta
,
using the running-time bounds from
Theorem

9
,
there is a function
f_{1}
such that the preprocessing in total takes time at most

f_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m)\cdot\left\lvert A\right\rvert^{1+\alpha}
.

For the enumeration, we iterate over all subsequences
S^{\prime}
of
S
with decreasing length,
starting with the longest subsequence
S^{\prime}=S
.
For every subsequence
S^{\prime}
, we iterate over all formulas
\varphi(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\Phi
,
in ascending lexicographic order on
(\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert,\left\lvert\varphi\right\rvert)
.
For every such formula, using the algorithm from
Theorem

9
9

based on our preprocessing above,
we iterate over all tuples
\bar{v}
that satisfy
\mathcal{A}_{S}\models\varphi_{S^{\prime}}[\bar{v}]
,
and we output
(\varphi,\bar{v},1-\left\lvert S^{\prime}\right\rvert/\left\lvert S\right\rvert)
for every such tuple.
Again, using the running-time bounds from
Theorem

9
,
there is a function
f_{2}
such that we enumerate the tuples with delay at most

f_{2}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m)
.
The statement of
Theorem

20
then follows with

f(\alpha,\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m)\coloneqq\max\bigl(f_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m),f_{2}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\delta,\sigma,I,m)\bigr)
.
∎
